23-00-00-040-801MMEL 23-16-1 (DDPG) Preparation - Control Wheel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches Inoperative ATA 23 · Task 040-801

Task Description

2. MMEL 23-16-1 (DDPG) Preparation - Control Wheel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the Control Wheel PTT switches inoperative. (2) The Control Wheel PTT switches can be inoperative under these conditions: (a) The associated Audio Control Panel PTT switch operates normally. (b) The inoperative switch is deactivated open.

B. References

Reference Title 23-51-04-000-801 Control Wheel PTT Switch Removal (P/B 401)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Confirmation

SUBTASK 23-00-00-700-003 (1) Do the steps that follow to confirm that the inoperative control wheel PTT switch is inoperative open: (a) Listen for a sound during a test transmission without pushing the associated PTT switch. NOTE: If there is sound, the PTT switch is inoperative in the closed position. (2) Use the audio selector panel PTT switch to verify R/T and/or I/C communications. 23-00-00

E. PTT Switches Deactivation

SUBTASK 23-00-00-860-001 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name C 21 C00560 INTERPHONE POWER F/O DC 2 C 22 C00561 INTERPHONE POWER F/O BAT C 23 C00239 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT DC 2 C 24 C00240 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT BAT D 22 C00086 AUDIO F/O D 23 C00083 AUDIO CAPT SUBTASK 23-00-00-010-001 (2) Remove the PTT switch on the control wheel to get access to the wire (TASK 23-51-04-000-801). SUBTASK 23-00-00-020-001 (3) Disconnect, cap and stow the GROUND (BLK/BRN) wire from the PTT switch. SUBTASK 23-00-00-420-001 (4) Install the PTT switch. SUBTASK 23-00-00-860-002 (5) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name C 21 C00560 INTERPHONE POWER F/O DC 2 C 22 C00561 INTERPHONE POWER F/O BAT C 23 C00239 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT DC 2 C 24 C00240 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT BAT D 22 C00086 AUDIO F/O D 23 C00083 AUDIO CAPT SUBTASK 23-00-00-930-001 (6) Put an INOP placard on the PTT switch.

23-00-00-440-801MMEL 23-16-1 (DDPG) Restoration - Control Wheel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches Inoperative ATA 23 · Task 440-801

Task Description

3. MMEL 23-16-1 (DDPG) Restoration - Control Wheel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the PTT switches inoperative.

B. References

Reference Title 23-51-04-000-801 Control Wheel PTT Switch Removal (P/B 401) 23-51-04-400-801 Control Wheel PTT Switch Installation (P/B 401) 23-00-00

C. Procedure

SUBTASK 23-00-00-860-003 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name C 21 C00560 INTERPHONE POWER F/O DC 2 C 22 C00561 INTERPHONE POWER F/O BAT C 23 C00239 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT DC 2 C 24 C00240 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT BAT D 22 C00086 AUDIO F/O D 23 C00083 AUDIO CAPT SUBTASK 23-00-00-020-002 (2) Remove the INOP placard from the switch. SUBTASK 23-00-00-020-003 (3) Remove the PTT switch on the control wheel (TASK 23-51-04-000-801). SUBTASK 23-00-00-020-004 (4) Remove the cap from the GROUND (BLK/BRN) wire for the PTT switch. SUBTASK 23-00-00-420-002 (5) Install the new PTT switch on the control wheel. To install it, do this task: Control Wheel PTT Switch Installation, TASK 23-51-04-400-801. SUBTASK 23-00-00-860-004 (6) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name C 21 C00560 INTERPHONE POWER F/O DC 2 C 22 C00561 INTERPHONE POWER F/O BAT C 23 C00239 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT DC 2 C 24 C00240 INTERPHONE POWER CAPT BAT D 22 C00086 AUDIO F/O D 23 C00083 AUDIO CAPT

23-00-00-040-807MMEL 23-16-2 (DDPG) Preparation - Flight Crew Audio Control Panel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches ATA 23 · Task 040-807

Task Description

4. MMEL 23-16-2 (DDPG) Preparation - Flight Crew Audio Control Panel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the PTT switches on the Flight Crew Audio Control Panel inoperative. (2) The Audio Control Panel PTT switches can be inoperative under these conditions: (a) The associated control wheel PTT switch operates normally (b) The inoperative switch is deactivated open. 23-00-00

B. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Confirmation

SUBTASK 23-00-00-700-001 (1) Do the steps that follow to confirm that the inoperative Audio Control Panel PTT switch is inoperative open and the associated control wheel PTT switch operates normally: (a) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811 (b) Push the PA microphone selector switch on the associated audio control panel. (c) Make sure that the PA MIC light comes on. (d) Speak into the associated microphone without pushing any PTT switch. (e) Make sure you hear no voice on the PA system. (f) Push the inoperative PTT switch and speak into the associated microphone. (g) Make sure you hear no voice at the other pilot's headset. (h) Push the associated pilot's control wheel PTT switch and speak into the associated microphone. (i) Make sure you hear your voice clearly at the other pilot's headset.

23-00-00-440-806MMEL 23-16-2 (DDPG) Restoration - Flight Crew Audio Control Panel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches ATA 23 · Task 440-806

Task Description

5. MMEL 23-16-2 (DDPG) Restoration - Flight Crew Audio Control Panel Push-To-Talk (PTT) Switches Inoperative

A. General

(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the Audio Control Panel PTT switches inoperative. (2) To put the airplane to usual condition, replace the Audio Control Panel with the inoperative switch.

B. References

Reference Title 23-51-02 P/B 401 AUDIO CONTROL PANEL - REMOVAL/INSTALLATION

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right 23-00-00

D. Procedure

SUBTASK 23-00-00-900-001 (1) Do the tasks: AUDIO CONTROL PANEL - REMOVAL/INSTALLATION, PAGEBLOCK 23-51-02/401

23-12-00-710-801VHF Communication System - Operational Test ATA 23 · Task 710-801

Task Description

2. VHF Communication System - Operational Test

A. General

(1) The operational test is a BITE test of the VHF communication transceiver. (a) The BITE test does a check of the internal functions of the VHF communication transceiver. The BITE test also does a check of the ARINC 429 frequency tuning input data to the VHF communication transceiver. RYR 006, 007, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-506; RYR 013, 014 PRE SB 737-23-1381; RYR 507-999 PRE SB 737-23-1673 (b) The same sequence of steps is used for the No. 1 or No. 2 VHF communication system. RYR 013, 014 POST SB 737-23-1381; RYR 043, 044 POST SB 737-23-1484; RYR 507-999 POST SB 737-23-1673 (c) The same sequence of steps is used for the No. 1, No. 2, or No. 3 VHF communication system. RYR ALL (d) The operational test is a quick test that makes sure that the VHF transceiver is functional.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Access Panels

Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door 23-12-00

D. VHF Communication System Operational Test

SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-003 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1 D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name C 10 C00284 PANEL & INSTR ELEX PANEL SUBTASK 23-12-00-010-001 (2) Open this access panel: Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH VHF TRANSCEIVER P/N 064-50000-XXXX SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-005 (3) Push and release one of the two TEST switches on the applicable VHF communication transceiver front panel to do the BITE test for each VHF communication system (VHF-1, VHF-2, or VHF-3). (a) Make sure that the LCD screen shows VHF DATA RADIO TEST IN PROGRESS. (b) If the LCD screen shows VHF DATA RADIO TEST COMPLETE - NO FAILURES, the BITE test passed. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH VHF TRANSCEIVER P/N 822-0693-XXX SUBTASK 23-12-00-740-002 (4) Do these steps on the applicable VHF communication transceiver front panel to do the BITE test for each VHF communication system (VHF-1, VHF-2, or VHF-3). (a) Push and hold the SQL/LAMP TEST switch. 1) Make sure that the red CONTROL INPUT FAIL light comes on. 2) Make sure that the green LRU PASS light comes on. (b) Release the SQL/LAMP TEST switch. (c) Push the TEST switch. 1) Make sure that the LRU PASS light comes on. 2) Make sure that the VSWR/power display shows less than 3.0. (d) Turn and hold the RFL/OFF/FWD switch in the FWD position. 1) Make sure that the VSWR/power display shows 25 or more for forward power. (e) Turn and hold the RFL/OFF/FWD switch in the RFL position. 23-12-00 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH VHF TRANSCEIVER P/N 822-0693-XXX (Continued) 1) Make sure that the VSWR/power display shows a RFL power which is less than one quarter of the forward power. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH VHF TRANSCEIVER P/N 822-1047-XXX OR 822-0323-XXX OR 822-1287-XXX SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-015 (5) Before you do the BITE test, make sure that the VHF-3 is in the voice mode (a voice frequency shows in the active (left) display of the RTP). SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-002 (6) Push and release the TEST switch on the applicable VHF communication transceiver front panel to do the BITE test for each VHF communication system (VHF-1, VHF-2, or VHF-3). (a) Make sure that these conditions occur: 1) All three LEDs on the VHF transceiver front panel turn red for about two seconds. 2) The LRU STATUS LED turns green and the CONTROL FAIL and ANTENNA FAIL LEDs remain red for about two seconds. 3) All three LEDs go off for about two seconds. 4) The LRU STATUS LED comes on green for about 30 seconds and the CONTROL FAIL and ANTENNA FAIL LEDs remain off. NOTE: Any of the three LEDs that come on red after the self-test is completed indicates a defective condition. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH VHF TRANSCEIVER P/N 965-1696-021 SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-014 (7) Push and release the TEST switch on the applicable VHF communication transceiver front panel to do the BITE test for each VHF communication system (VHF-1, VHF-2, or VHF-3). (a) The VDR STATUS LED will turn on green and the CONTROL FAULT and ANTENNA FAULT LEDs remain off. NOTE: Any of the three LEDs that come on red or amber after the self-test is completed indicates a defective condition. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH VHF TRANSCEIVER P/N 822-1250-XXX SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-016 (8) Before you do the BITE test, make sure that the VHF-3 is in the voice mode (a voice frequency shows in the active (left) display of the RTP). SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-007 (9) Push and release the TEST switch on the applicable VHF communication transceiver front panel to do the BITE test for each VHF communication system (VHF-1, VHF-2, or VHF-3). (a) Make sure that these conditions occur: 1) All three LEDs on the VHF transceiver front panel turn red for about two seconds. 2) The LRU STATUS LED turns green and the CONTROL FAIL and ANTENNA FAIL LEDs remain red for about two seconds. 3) All three LEDs go off for about five seconds. 23-12-00 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH VHF TRANSCEIVER P/N 822-1250-XXX (Continued) 4) The LRU STATUS LED comes on green for about 30 seconds and the CONTROL FAIL and ANTENNA FAIL LEDs remain off. NOTE: Any of the three LEDs that come on red after the self-test is completed indicates a defective condition. RYR ALL

SUBTASK 23-12-00-410-001 (1) Close this access panel: Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

23-12-00-730-801VHF Communication System - System Test ATA 23 · Task 730-801

Task Description

3. VHF Communication System - System Test

A. General

(1) This task includes these tests: (a) A Built-In-Test Equipment (BITE) Test (b) An Radio Tuning Panel (RTP) to RTP Interface Test (c) A Very High Frequency (VHF) Communication Test. (2) Make sure that the flight interphone system operates. The communication test uses these flight interphone system components: Audio Control Panel (ACP)s, headsets, microphones, and Push-To-T alk (PTT) switches. (3) Make sure that the airplane is not in or near any large metal structures. The position of the airplane on the ground can decrease the quality of VHF communication. Ground equipment and structures can also cause blocked Radio Frequency (RF) signal attenuation. If the quality of transmitted and received voice signals is not satisfactory, move the airplane to a better position. (4) Make sure that you use approved test frequencies when you speak with the radio tower operator.

B. References

Reference Title 23-27-00-700-812-009 ACARS - System Test (P/B 501) 23-27-00-700-815-001 ACARS - System Test (P/B 501)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right 23-12-00

D. Prepare for the Test

SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-009 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1 D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name C 10 C00284 PANEL & INSTR ELEX PANEL SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-023 (2) If it is necessary, do these steps to change from Data to Voice: (a) Push the applicable VHF key on RTP-1 (or, on RTP-2) on the P8 panel. (b) Tune the applicable RTP to display “DATA” in the active window. (c) Push the display transfer button on the RTP . (d) Make sure that “DATA” is in the standby window of the RTP . SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-024 (3) If it is necessary, do these steps to change from Voice to Data: (a) Push the display transfer button on the RTP . (b) Make sure that “DATA” toggles from the standby display to the active display on the RTP . SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-025 (4) If it is necessary, do these steps to change back from Data to Voice: (a) Push the display transfer button on the RTP . (b) Make sure that “DATA” toggles from the active display to the standby display on the RTP .

E. BITE Test

SUBTASK 23-12-00-710-003 (1) Do this task: VHF Communication System - Operational Test, TASK 23-12-00-710-801. F. RTP to RTP Interface Test SUBTASK 23-12-00-730-003 (1) Do these steps at the RTPs: (a) Push the VHF3 switch on each RTP-1. (b) Push the VHF3 switch on each RTP-2. 1) Make sure that RTP-1, and RTP-2 show the same ACTIVE and STANDBY frequencies. (c) Set RTP-2 to a new frequency. 1) Make sure that RTP-1 shows the new frequency. 23-12-00 (d) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are on, on RTP-1 and RTP-2. NOTE: The cross tuning light is above the display transfer switch, between the frequency displays. (e) Push the VHF2 switch on RTP-2. 1) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are off, on RTP-2. 2) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are on, on RTP-1. (f) Push the VHF1 switch on RTP-1. 1) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are off, on RTP-1. (g) Push the HF1 switch on RTP-2. 1) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are on, on RTP-1 and RTP-2. NOTE: The cross tuning light is above the display transfer switch, between the frequency displays. (h) Push the HF2 switch on RTP-2. (i) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are off, on RTP-1 and RTP-2. (j) Push the HF2 switch on RTP-1. (k) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are on, on RTP-1 and RTP-2. NOTE: The cross tuning light is above the display transfer switch, between the frequency displays. (l) Push the HF1 switch on RTP-1. (m) Make sure that the cross tuning lights are off, on RTP-1 and RTP-2.

G. VHF Communication Test

SUBTASK 23-12-00-730-004 (1) Do a test of the VHF-1 communication system: (a) Connect a headset/boom microphone to the captain's jack panel. (b) Do these steps to prepare the captain's ACP for VHF-1 system operation: 1) Push and release the VHF1 switch. a) Make sure that the switch light comes on. 2) Push and release the VHF-1 volume control. a) Make sure that the volume control indicator light comes on. 3) Set the volume control to the middle position. 4) Set the MASK/BOOM switch (if installed) to the BOOM position. (c) Set the RTP-1 to an approved test frequency for VHF-1. (d) Do these steps to do a voice communication test with a radio tower operator: 1) Push and hold a PTT switch while you speak. a) Make sure that you hear the sidetone in the headset while you speak. 2) Release the PTT switch while you listen. a) Make sure that the quality of the transmitted and received voice is satisfactory. b) Do this step to make sure that the volume of the received voice will change: turn the VHF1 volume control on the captain's ACP with no change in voice quality. 23-12-00 <1> Turn the VHF1 volume control on the captain's ACP . RYR 097-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999 (e) Do these steps to do a 35 second keyline timer test: 1) Make sure that you have clearance from the tower operator to transmit for 40 seconds. 2) Push and hold the PTT switch for a minimum of 40 seconds. a) Make sure that after about 35 seconds, the sidetone is not present in the headset. 3) Release the PTT switch. RYR ALL SUBTASK 23-12-00-730-005 (2) Do a test of the VHF-2 communication system: (a) Connect a headset/boom microphone to the first officer's jack panel. (b) Do these steps to prepare the first officer's ACP for VHF-2 system operation: 1) Push and release the VHF2 switch. a) Make sure that the switch light comes on. 2) Push and release the VHF2 volume control. a) Make sure that the volume control indicator light comes on. 3) Set the volume control to the middle position. 4) Set the MASK/BOOM switch (if installed) to the BOOM position. (c) Set the RTP-2 to an approved test frequency for VHF-2. (d) Do these steps to do a voice communication test with a radio tower operator: 1) Push and hold a PTT switch while you speak. a) Make sure that you hear the sidetone in the headset while you speak. 2) Release the PTT switch while you listen. a) Make sure that the quality of the transmitted and received voice is satisfactory. b) Do this step to make sure that the volume of the received voice will change: <1> Turn the VHF2 volume control on the first officer's ACP . RYR 097-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999 (e) Do these steps to do a 35 second keyline timer test: 1) Make sure that you have clearance from the tower operator to transmit for 40 seconds. 2) Push and hold the PTT switch for a minimum of 40 seconds. a) Make sure that after about 35 seconds, the sidetone is not present in the headset. 3) Release the PTT switch. (b) Do these steps to prepare the captain's ACP for VHF-3 system operation: 1) Push and release the VHF3 switch. a) Make sure that the switch light comes on. 2) Push and release the VHF3 volume control. a) Make sure that the volume control indicator light comes on. 3) Set the volume control to the middle position. 4) Set the MASK/BOOM switch (if installed) to the BOOM position. H. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-015 (1) At each ACP , push and release a different transmitter selector switch that is not the applicable VHF switch. 23-12-00 (a) Make sure that the related VHF switch light goes off. NOTE: When you push a different transmitter selector switch, on an ACP , the first transmitter switch mode is cancelled. SUBTASK 23-12-00-860-017 (2) At each ACP , push and release the applicable VHF volume controls. (a) Make sure that all the volume control indicator lights go off.

2. VHF Communication Antenna - Removal (Figure 401)

A. Tools/Equipment

Zone Area RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; RYR 043, 044 POST SB 737-23-1484 23-12-11 RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; RYR 043, 044 POST SB 737-23-1484 (Continued) (Continued) Zone Area 123 Forward Cargo Compartment - Left RYR ALL 143 Area Below Aft Cargo Compartment - Left 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right 231 Forward Passenger Compartment - Forward Entry Door to Sta 663.75 - Left

C. Removal Procedure

SUBTASK 23-12-11-860-008 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1 D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2 SUBTASK 23-12-11-020-001 (2) Do these steps to remove the VHF communication antenna [1]: (a) Remove the ten bolts [2] that attach the VHF communication antenna [1] to the airplane. CAUTION REMOVE THE AERODYNAMIC FILLET SEAL CAREFULLY WITH THE SEALANT REMOVAL TOOL. DAMAGE TO THE AIRPLANE SKIN OR THE COAXIAL CABLE CAN OCCUR. (b) While you hold the VHF communication antenna [1], use a sealant removal tool, COM-2481 to break the fillet seal around the base of the antenna. CAUTION DO NOT PULL ON THE COAXIAL CABLE. CAREFULLY MOVE THE ANTENNA. DISCONNECT THE COAXIAL CONNECTOR. THIS WILL PREVENT DAMAGE TO THE COAXIAL CABLE. (c) Move the VHF communication antenna [1] away from the airplane to get access to the coaxial connector [4] on the coaxial cable. (d) Disconnect the coaxial connector [4] from the VHF communication antenna [1] and remove the antenna. NOTE: For the top VHF communication antenna [1], make sure the coaxial connector [4] does not go back through the hole in the fuselage. Make sure you attach the coaxial connector [4] to something to prevent this.

23-12-11-000-802VHF Communication Antenna With Gasket - Removal ATA 23 · Task 000-802

Task Description

3. VHF Communication Antenna With Gasket - Removal Figure 402

A. General

(1) This procedure is used for the removal of antennas that use HI-TEK or Aerobond gaskets per 737-6789 SL-20-045. (2) As full certification tests of the gaskets was not done, it is recommended to do the steps that follow: (a) Do a visual inspection of the gasket and the mating surface each D check. (b) Do a check for deterioration and corrosion on the antenna and gasket after their removal. (c) Do a check of the airplane skin for corrosion and fretting caused by the antenna gasket. 1) Remove any corrosion and fretting on the airplane skin.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; RYR 043, 044 POST SB 737-23-1484 123 Forward Cargo Compartment - Left RYR ALL 143 Area Below Aft Cargo Compartment - Left 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right 231 Forward Passenger Compartment - Forward Entry Door to Sta 663.75 - Left

C. Removal Procedure

SUBTASK 23-12-11-860-012 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1 D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2 SUBTASK 23-12-11-020-002 (2) Do these steps to remove the VHF communication antenna [1] : (a) Remove the ten bolts [2] that attach the VHF communication antenna [1] to the airplane. (b) Move the VHF communication antenna [1] away from the airplane to get access to the coaxial connector [4] on the coaxial cable. 23-12-11 CAUTION DO NOT PULL ON THE ANTENNA COAXIAL CABLE. CAREFULLY MOVE THE VHF COMMUNICATION ANTENNA. DISCONNECT THE COAXIAL CONNECTOR. THIS WILL PREVENT DAMAGE TO THE COAXIAL CABLE. (c) Disconnect the coaxial connector [4] from the VHF communication antenna [1] and remove the antenna. NOTE: For the top VHF communication antenna [1], make sure the coaxial connector does not go back through the hole in the fuselage. Make sure you attach the coaxial connector to something to prevent this. (d) Remove and discard the gasket [5].

Zone Area RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; RYR 043, 044 POST SB 737-23-1484 123 Forward Cargo Compartment - Left RYR ALL 143 Area Below Aft Cargo Compartment - Left 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right 231 Forward Passenger Compartment - Forward Entry Door to Sta 663.75 - Left F. Prepare for the Installation SUBTASK 23-12-11-860-006 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are open and have safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1 D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2 23-12-11 SUBTASK 23-12-11-100-002 (2) Clean the airplane mating surface. (a) Remove sealant with a sealant removal tool, COM-2481. WARNING DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS ARE DANGEROUS MATERIALS. SOLVENTS CAN BE FLAMMABLE. OBEY THE MATERIAL SAFETY DATA SHEETS (MSDS) FOR SOLVENTS. OBEY LOCAL REGULATIONS FOR THE CORRECT PROCEDURES TO USE OR DISCARD SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (b) Clean the airplane mating surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with solvent, B00148, solvent, B00666, solvent, B00184, FCC-55 solvent, B01026, or solvent, B01054. (c) Use a clean cotton wiper, G00034, to clean the airplane mating surface. (d) Make sure that the airplane mating surface is dry. SUBTASK 23-12-11-100-003 (3) If the mating surface has corrosion or other damage, or if the airplane mating surface has no chemical conversion coating, do these steps to prepare the airplane mating surface: NOTE: An alternative installation procedure is to apply corrosion inhibiting compound, G00009, on an airplane mating surface that has no chemical conversion coating. (a) If there is corrosion, do this task: Removal and Control of Corrosion for Aluminum and Aluminum Alloys, TASK 51-21-31-350-801. (b) Apply a layer of Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005, to the airplane mating surface (TASK 51-21-41-370-802). NOTE: Alternative chemical conversion coatings may also be used (TASK 51-21-41-370-802). SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-001 (4) Install a new o-ring [3]. (a) If it is necessary, lubricate the o-ring [3] with Parker Super-O-Lube lubricant, D00652. (b) Align the o-ring [3] in the groove.

G. VHF Communication Antenna Installation

SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-002 (1) Keep one of the 10 bolts [2] out to do an electrical bond check. SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-006 (2) Install the VHF communication antenna [1]. (a) Connect the coaxial connector [4] to the VHF communication antenna [1]. (b) Apply a layer of sealant, A00247, to the shank and threads of 9 of the 10 bolts [2]. (c) Put the VHF communication antenna [1] in its position and install 9 of the bolts [2]. 1) Tighten the bolts [2] to 80 ±2 in-lb (9.0 ±0.2 N·m). SUBTASK 23-12-11-700-001 (3) Measure the resistance between the VHF communication antenna [1] base and the airplane skin (SWPM 20-20-00). NOTE: Use the empty bolt hole to get access to the VHF communication antenna base. 23-12-11 (a) Use a intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550. (b) Make sure that the resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0 milliohm) or less. SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-003 (4) Apply a layer of sealant, A00247, to the shank and threads of the last bolt [2]. (a) Install the last bolt [2]. 1) Tighten the bolt [2] to 80 ±2 in-lb (9.0 ±0.2 N·m). SUBTASK 23-12-11-390-001 (5) Apply a layer of sealant, A00247, around the base of the VHF communication antenna [1] to make a corrosion fillet seal (TASK 51-31-00-390-804). NOTE: Operators can defer the application of the aero-sealant in the antenna installation until next “A” check or overnight maintenance stop to prevent a flight delay. The deferral of the application of sealant must not exceed a maximum of eight weeks (SL 20-043). (a) Let the sealant dry for the correct cure time. SUBTASK 23-12-11-390-002 (6) Fill the extra holes on the antenna base with sealant, A00247. NOTE: Additional holes on the antenna base were once intended to be used for jack screws to help with the removal of the antenna. This is no longer recommended. Jack screws could damage the airplane skin. SUBTASK 23-12-11-860-007 (7) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1 D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2

H. Installation Test

SUBTASK 23-12-11-730-001 (1) Do this task: VHF Communication System - System Test, TASK 23-12-00-730-801.

DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS ARE DANGEROUS MATERIALS. SOLVENTS CAN BE FLAMMABLE. OBEY THE MATERIAL SAFETY DATA SHEETS (MSDS) FOR SOLVENTS. OBEY LOCAL REGULATIONS FOR THE CORRECT PROCEDURES TO USE OR DISCARD SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (a) Clean the airplane mating surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with solvent, B00148, solvent, B00666, solvent, B00184, FCC-55 solvent, B01026, or solvent, B01054. H. VHF Communication Antenna With Gasket Installation

SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-004 (1) Do these steps to install the VHF communication antenna [1]: (a) Remove and discard the O-ring (if installed) from the VHF communication antenna [1]. (b) Remove the protective film from each side of the new gasket [5]. (c) Put the gasket [5] on the VHF communication antenna [1]. (d) Connect the coaxial connector [4] to the VHF communication antenna [1]. (e) Apply a layer of sealant, A00247, to the shank and threads of the 10 bolts [2]. CAUTION DO NOT APPLY SEALANT TO THE EDGES OF THE ANTENNA. THE GASKET RELEASES THE MOISTURE. IF YOU SEAL THE EDGES, THE MOISTURE WILL STAY . THIS CAN CAUSE CORROSION DAMAGE TO THE AIRPLANE, AND ANTENNA MATING SURFACES. (f) Put the VHF communication antenna [1] in its position and install 9 of the 10 bolts [2]. 1) If the bolts [2] are installed in domed nut plates, remove the elastomer from the mounting holes in the gasket. 2) Tighten the bolts [2] to 80 ±2 in-lb (9 ±0 N·m). NOTE: The gel material of the gasket is compressible, the bolts need to be re-torqued 15 minutes of the installation. (g) Fill extra holes on the antenna base with sealant, A00247. NOTE: Extra holes on the antenna base were once intended to be used for jack screws to help remove the antenna. This is no longer recommended. Jack screws could damage the airplane skin. SUBTASK 23-12-11-700-002 (2) Measure the resistance between the VHF communication antenna [1] base and the airplane skin with a intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550. (a) Make sure that the resistance is 0.001 ohm or less. SUBTASK 23-12-11-420-005 (3) Install the last bolt [2] to 80 ±2 in-lb (9 ±0 N·m). (a) Tighten the bolt [2] to 80 ±2 in-lb (9 ±0 N·m). NOTE: The gel material of the gasket is compressible, the bolts need to be re-torqued 15 minutes of the installation. 23-12-11 SUBTASK 23-12-11-860-014 (4) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1 D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2

D. VHF Communication Antenna Inspection

SUBTASK 23-12-11-210-001 (1) Visually inspect VHF antenna for damage or unusual wear. (a) Make sure that all mating surfaces are clean and carefully fitted. 1) If you find any indications of cracking or delamination, replace the VHF antenna: • VHF Communication Antenna With Gasket - Removal, TASK 23-12-11-000-802 • VHF Communication Antenna With Gasket - Installation, TASK 23-12-11-400-802 • VHF Communication Antenna - Removal, TASK 23-12-11-000-801 • VHF Communication Antenna - Installation, TASK 23-12-11-400-801. (b) Make sure that the paint erosion on the leading edge is less than 1 in. (25.40 mm) back. 1) If the paint erosion is down to the primer, repaint the antenna as follows: NOTE: Figure 601 shows an example of caution markings for the bottom VHF antenna. Caution markings for the upper VHF antenna are the same. Follow airline requirements for VHF antenna marking. A HIGH VSWR WILL OCCUR. THIS CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE VHF COMM TRANSCEIVER. b) Apply coating, C00033, Enamel or equivalent. c) If applicable, apply paint stripes on the bottom or top VHF antenna (Figure 601).

SUBTASK 23-12-41-020-001 CAUTION CLEARLY IDENTIFY THE CONNECTOR FOR THE RTP POSITION (NO. 1, NO. 2 OR NO. 3) DURING THE REPLACEMENT . IF NOT CAREFUL, A CROSS CONNECTION CAN OCCUR DURING INSTALLATION. 23-12-41-800-802Radio Tuning Panel - INOP Display Toggle ATA 23 · Task 800-802

Task Description

4. Radio Tuning Panel - INOP Display Toggle

A. General

(1) You can use this procedure to set or remove INOP from RTP ACTIVE/STANDBY frequency displays. If the RTP frequency displays are normal, this procedure will change the displays to show INOP . If the RTP frequency displays show INOP , this procedure will change the frequency displays to normal. NOTE: For airplanes with the Gables G7404-04 RTP installed, there is a known software problem that causes INOP to show, even though the INOP pin option is not enabled. This procedure can be used to remove the INOP indication for those airplanes. Boeing recommends that the G7404-04 be replaced by the G7404-24 or G7404-124 panel at the airlines convenience. (2) If a communication system is not installed, you can use this procedure to set the RTP to INOP for the applicable communication system. 23-12-41 (a) For Gables P/N G7404-0X, the RTP can be set to show INOP for VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or HF2. (b) For Gables P/N G7404-2X or G7404-12X, the RTP can be set to show INOP only for VHF3, HF1 or HF2. (c) When the display for a communication system is changed in one RTP , the display of all RTP's will be changed for that communication system. (d) If this procedure is used to set frequency displays from INOP to normal, a communication system failure will change (toggle) the applicable communication system to FAIL or back to INOP . (e) When RTP frequency displays show INOP , the set communication system is not on or not installed. (f) When RTP frequency displays show FAIL, the set communication system has failed. (g) When RTP frequency displays show PANEL FAIL, there is a problem in the RTP , not in a communication system. The RTP self test failed.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Radio Tuning Panel - Display Toggle Procedure

SUBTASK 23-12-41-860-023 (1) Do these steps: (a) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1 D 12 C00471 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 3 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name C 3 C00166 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 2 (b) Push the RTP-1 Radio Tuning switch (VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or HF2) for the communication system displays to be changed. (c) Push the same Radio Tuning switch on RTP-2 (VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or HF2). (d) Push the same Radio Tuning switch on RTP-3 (if installed) (VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or HF2). (e) Open this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1 (f) Wait a minimum of 5 seconds. (g) Push and hold down the RTP-1 Frequency Transfer switch. (h) Push and hold down the RTP-1 Radio Tuning switch (VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or HF2) for the communication system displays to be changed. 23-12-41 (i) While still holding the Frequency Transfer switch and Radio Tuning system switch on RTP-1, Close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name D 11 C00165 COMMUNICATIONS VHF 1 (j) Make sure RTP-1, RTP-2, and RTP-3 (if installed) displays have changed (toggled). (k) Release the RTP-1 Frequency Transfer switch. (l) Release the Radio Tuning switch (VHF1, VHF2, VHF3, HF1 or HF2).

23-15-40-710-801Iridium SATCOM System - Operational Test ATA 23 · Task 710-801

Task Description

2. Iridium SATCOM System - Operational Test

A. General

(1) This task gives the instructions to do an operational test of the Iridium Satellite Communications System (SATCOM) system. (2) This test includes these tests: (a) SATCOM BITE Test (b) Satellite Link Test (c) SATCOM Voice Communication Test (d) SATCOM Link Test (3) The airplane must be outside of the hangar and away from any structures that may block the view of the Iridium satellites from the antenna.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Access Panels

Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

D. Prepare Procedure

SUBTASK 23-15-40-010-001 (1) Open this access panel: Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

E. SATCOM BITE Test

SUBTASK 23-15-40-710-001 (1) Open this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name E 13 C04060 SATCOM SDU This circuit breaker is inoperative and should remain open: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name RYR 509-518 PRE SB 737-23-1670 AND POST SB 737-23-1724 23-15-40 RYR 509-518 PRE SB 737-23-1670 AND POST SB 737-23-1724 (Continued) (Continued) F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name D 2 C00857 COMMUNICATIONS HF 2 (INOP) RYR 507, 508, 519-999; RYR 509-518 POST SB 737-23-1724 (2) Make sure that the SAT prompt does not show on the Control Display Unit (CDU). NOTE: It may take approximately one minute to display the message. (3) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name E 13 C04060 SATCOM SDU This circuit breaker is inoperative and should remain open: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name RYR 509-518 PRE SB 737-23-1670 AND POST SB 737-23-1724 D 2 C00857 COMMUNICATIONS HF 2 (INOP) RYR 507, 508, 519-999; RYR 509-518 POST SB 737-23-1724 (4) Make sure that the SAT prompt shows on the CDU. (5) Wait for the Satellite Data Unit (SDU) to power-up. NOTE: It can take approximately up to 90 seconds for the SDU to power up. (6) After the SDU powered up, do these steps to examine the SATCOM BITE status: (a) Make sure that the LEDs on the front panel of the SDU show status as follows: 1) POWER: Green 2) LBT-1: Green (steady/flashing) 3) LBT-2: Amber (steady/flashing) 4) SBD: Green (steady/flashing) 5) ARINC: Green (steady/flashing) (b) Do these steps on all CDUs: 1) Push the MENU key on all CDUs keypads to show the CDUs “MENU” page. 2) Make sure that the <SAT prompt shows on the MENU page on all the CDUs. 3) Select the Line Select Key (LSK) adjacent to <SATon all CDUs to show the SAT-PHONE 1/2 page. 4) Push the NEXT PAGE key on all CDUs keypads to show the SAT-PHONE 2/2 page. 5) On the SAT-PHONE 2/2 page of all CDUs, make sure that BITE OK> shows at LSK-2R. 6) Push the MENU key on all CDUs keypads to show the CDU “MENU” page. 23-15-40 F. Satellite Link Test SUBTASK 23-15-40-710-002 (1) Do the steps that follow to do a satellite link test: NOTE: This procedure verifies the connection between the antenna, the SDU and the satellite link. (a) On one of the CDUs, push the MENU key on the CDU keypad to show the CDU “MENU” page. (b) Select the LSK adjacent to <SAT to show the SAT-PHONE 1/2 page. (c) Push the NEXT PAGE key on the CDU keypad to show the SAT-PHONE 2/2 page. (d) Select the LSK adjacent to <LOG to show the SAT LOG page. (e) Examine the MODEM Status on the SAT LOG page. 1) Make sure that the MODEM status shows as follows: a) LBT-1 (VOICE) READY b) LBT-2 (VOICE) DISABLED c) SBD (DATA) READY d) Make sure that the SIGNAL level of LBT-1, LBT-2 and SBD stays above 3. (f) Select the LSK adjacent to <RETURN to show the SAT-PHONE 2/2 page.

G. SATCOM Voice Communication Test

SUBTASK 23-15-40-710-003 (1) Do the steps that follow to do a SATCOM voice communication test for Air-to-Ground call from the captain's station: NOTE: This test verifies the interface between the SDU and the audio system, and also verifies the SIM card activation status. (a) Do a telephone number entry at the CDU-L: 1) Push the NEXT PAGE key on the CDU keypad to show the SAT-PHONE 1/2 page. 2) Select the LSK adjacent to DIRECTORY> to show the SAT DIRECTORY INDEX page. 3) Select the LSK adjacent to COMPANY> to show the AOC DIR DETAILS for COMPANY page. 4) Select the LSK-L adjacent to the telephone number that is authorized to do this test. 5) Make sure that the selected telephone number's name/ID is entered below the SAT-1 “<MAKE CALL” (b) Do these steps at the captain's Audio Control Panel (ACP): 1) Push the applicable audio volume controls to make all the receive lights go off. 2) Push the SAT-1 microphone selector switch. 3) Turn on the SAT-1 volume control and set it to approximately the middle position. (c) Do these steps at the First Officer (F/O)s ACP: 1) Turn off the SAT-1 volume control. (d) Set the volume knob on the captain's cockpit speaker to the middle level or normal comfortable listening setting. 23-15-40 (e) On CDU-L, select the LSK adjacent to <MAKE CALL for SAT-1. NOTE: The status of SAT-1 changes from READY - DIALING - RINGING - ANSWERED. (f) When the call is connected, make sure that the CALL light for SAT-1 comes on at each ACP . (g) Make voice communication from captain's station with a handheld microphone and cockpit speakers. NOTE: You must push the Push-to-T alk (PTT) when you talk or transmit your voice. (h) Make sure that the audio quality from both sides (cockpit and other end) is good. NOTE: A delay may exist and it is not considered a problem. Any unacceptable level of noise may be caused by the audio wire installation. In this condition, end the call by selecting <END CALL on the CDU, and repeat this procedure with a different telephone number, and verify the audio quality. If the audio quality improves with the new telephone number, then this is not a problem. (i) T ell the ground operator that the call will be completed. (j) To end the call, select the LSK adjacent to <END CALL for SAT-1 on the CDU-L. 1) Make sure that the CALL light for SAT-1 goes off at each ACP . (k) On captain's ACP , push the SAT-1 microphone selector switch to make the MIC light go off. SUBTASK 23-15-40-710-004 (2) Do the steps that follow to do a SATCOM voice communication test for Ground-to-Air call from the F/Os station: (a) Do these steps at the F/Os ACP: 1) Push the SAT-1 microphone selector switch. 2) Turn on the SAT-1 volume control and set it to approximately the middle position. (b) Do these steps at the Captain's ACP: 1) Turn off the SAT-1 volume control. 2) Push the VHF-1 microphone selector switch. 3) Turn on the VHF-1 volume control and set it to approximately the middle position. (c) Set the volume knob on the F/Os cockpit speaker to the middle level or normal comfortable listening setting. (d) Use VHF-1 radio to tell the ground operator to initiate a SATCOM call. (e) Make sure that a single tone chime is heard when the Ground-to-Air call is received. (f) Make sure that the CALL light for SAT-1 comes on at each ACP . (g) On CDU-L, make sure that the status of SAT-1 shows ANSWERED. (h) Make voice communication from F/Os station with a handheld microphone and cockpit speakers. NOTE: You must push the PTT when you talk or transmit your voice. (i) Make sure that the audio quality from both sides (cockpit and other end) is good. NOTE: A delay may exist and it is not considered a problem. Any unacceptable level of noise may be caused by the audio wire installation. In this condition, end the call by selecting <END CALL on the CDU, and repeat this procedure with a different telephone number, and verify the audio quality. If the audio quality improves with the new telephone number, then this is not a problem. 23-15-40 (j) T ell the ground operator that the call will be completed. (k) To end the call, select the LSK adjacent to <END CALL for SAT-1 on the CDU-L. 1) Make sure that the CALL light for SAT-1 goes off at each ACP . (l) On F/Os ACP , push the SAT-1 microphone selector switch to make the MIC light go off.

H. SATCOM Link Test

SUBTASK 23-15-40-710-005 (1) Use the Aircraft Communications Addressing And Reporting System (ACARS) to do a SATCOM Link Test: NOTE: This test verifies the interface between the SDU and ACARS Communications Management Unit (CMU). (a) Push the MENU key on one of the CDUs to show the CDU “MENU” page. 1) Make sure that the <DLK prompt shows. NOTE: It may take approximately up to 1 minute to display the <DLK” prompt after the CMU is powered on. (b) Select the LSK adjacent to <DLK to show the DATALINK (DLK) MENU page. NOTE: If the CDU displays a page other than the DATALINK (DLK) MENU page, select the LSK adjacent to the <RETURN" prompt until the DATALINK (DLK) MENU page is displayed. (c) Select the LSK adjacent to the MAINT> to show the CMU MAINTENANCE MENU page. (d) Select the LSK adjacent to the <TEST to show the CMU TEST page. (e) Select the LSKadjacent to the <INITIATE prompt under the SAT LINK prompt. (f) Make sure that the word INITIATE, then the word TEST , then the word PASS, then the word INITIATE shows on the display. (g) Select the LSK adjacent to the <RETURN prompt until the DATALINK (DLK) MENU page is displayed. (h) Push the MENU key on the CDU keypad to show the CDU “MENU” page.

I. Put the Airplane in Its Usual Condition

2. Iridium SATCOM System - SIM Card Replacement (Figure 201)

A. General

D. Access Panels

Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

E. Prepare Procedure

SUBTASK 23-15-42-860-002 (1) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name E 13 C04060 SATCOM SDU This circuit breaker is inoperative and should remain open: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name RYR 509-518 PRE SB 737-23-1670 AND POST SB 737-23-1724 D 2 C00857 COMMUNICATIONS HF 2 (INOP) RYR 507, 508, 519-999; RYR 509-518 POST SB 737-23-1724 SUBTASK 23-15-42-010-003 (2) Open this access panel: Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door 23-15-42 F. SIM Card Removal SUBTASK 23-15-42-010-004 (1) Open the coverplate from the CIM to prepare for the removal of SIM cards. SUBTASK 23-15-42-020-002 (2) Do these steps to remove the SIM card [1] out of the SIM CARD SLOT-1 and SIM CARD SLOT-2: (a) Slide the SIM card lock [2] on each SIM card holder [3] up to the unlocked position. NOTE: This will unlock the SIM card holder and let it be hinged upward. (b) Lift up the two SIM card holder [3]. (c) Remove the SIM card [1] from the SIM card holder [3]. (d) If no other task is necessary, do these steps: 1) Push the SIM card holders down. 2) Slide the SIM card lock [2] on each SIM card holder down until the holder clicks to the locked position. 3) Close the CIM coverplate.

G. SIM Card Installation

SUBTASK 23-15-42-420-001 (1) To install the SIM card [1] into the CIM, do the steps that follow: (a) Open the coverplate from the CIM (if closed). (b) Do these steps to install the SIM card [1] into the SIM CARD SLOT-1 and SIM CARD SLOT-2: 1) Slide the SIM card lock [2] on each SIM card holder [3] up to the unlocked position. NOTE: This will unlock the SIM card holder and let it be hinged upward. 2) Lift up the two SIM card holder [3]. 3) Make sure that the clipped corner of each SIM card [1] is in the correct position, as shown in the Figure 201. 4) Install the SIM card [1] into the SIM card holder [3]. 5) Push the SIM card holder [3] down. 6) Slide the SIM card lock [2] on each SIM card holder [3] down until the holder clicks to the locked position. SUBTASK 23-15-42-860-003 (2) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name E 13 C04060 SATCOM SDU This circuit breaker is inoperative and should remain open: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name RYR 509-518 PRE SB 737-23-1670 AND POST SB 737-23-1724 D 2 C00857 COMMUNICATIONS HF 2 (INOP) RYR 507, 508, 519-999; RYR 509-518 POST SB 737-23-1724 23-15-42 SUBTASK 23-15-42-210-001 (3) Make sure the three LEDs in between the SIM cards come on Green. NOTE: The CIM Module contains three LEDs. These LEDs are power indicators for the Configuration Board, SIM slot 1, and SIM slot 2. The LEDs have two states, ON (green) or OFF (no color), to indicate power supplied to the unit. SUBTASK 23-15-42-410-001 (4) Close the CIM coverplate. SUBTASK 23-15-42-410-002 (5) Close this access panel: Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

H. Installation Test

SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-001 (1) Use the center CDU to do the SIM card installation test: NOTE: The airplane must be outside of the hangar and away from any structures that may block the view of the Iridium satellites from the antenna. (a) Push the MENU key on the CDU keypad to show the CDU “MENU” page. (b) Select the LSK adjacent to <SAT to show the SAT-PHONE 1/2 page. (c) Push the NEXT PAGE key on the CDU keypad to show the SAT-PHONE 2/2 page. (d) Select the LSK adjacent to <LOG to show the SAT LOG page. (e) Examine the MODEM Status on the SAT LOG page. 1) Make sure that these conditions show: a) LBT-1 (VOICE) “READY” b) LBT-2 (VOICE) “READY” c) SBD (DATA) “READY” d) Make sure that the SIGNAL level of LBT-1, LBT-2 and SBD stays above 3. (f) Select the LSK adjacent to <RETURN to show the SAT-PHONE 2/2 page. (g) Select the LSK adjacent to CONFIG> to show the SAT CONFIG 1/2 page. (h) Push the NEXT PAGE key on the CDU keypad to show the SAT CONFIG 2/2 page. (i) Make sure that the SIM and IMEI numbers for LBT-1 (for SIM 1) and LBT-2 (for SIM 2) agree with the record from the Iridium service application. (j) Select the LSK adjacent to <RETURN to show the SAT-PHONE 2/2 page. (k) Push the MENU key on the CDU keypad to return back to the CDU “MENU” page. SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-002 (2) Do the SATCOM voice communication test in AMM Iridium SATCOM System - Operational Test, TASK 23-15-40-710-801.

23-15-42-860-801Iridium SATCOM System - User Configuration Verifications and Changes ATA 23 · Task 860-801

Task Description

3. Iridium SATCOM System - User Configuration Verifications and Changes (Figure 202, Figure 203)

A. General

(1) This task includes the steps to check the customer specific SATCOM configuration.

B. References

Reference Title 32-09-00 P/B 201 AIR/GROUND SYSTEM - MAINTENANCE PRACTICES

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Prepare for the Configuration Verification and Changes

SUBTASK 23-15-42-860-009 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name C 16 C01356 LANDING GEAR AIR/GND SYS 1 D 1 C01399 PSEU PRI D 2 C01400 PSEU ALTN SUBTASK 23-15-42-980-001 (2) Make sure that the airplane is in the ground mode (PAGEBLOCK 32-09-00/201). SUBTASK 23-15-42-580-001 (3) Set the parking brakes.

E. User Configuration Verifications and Changes

SUBTASK 23-15-42-860-007 (1) Read the procedure carefully before you make changes in the SAT USER ORT and SAT CONFIG DB PN pages. NOTE: You can cause a malfunction of the SATCOM system if you install the incorrect configuration. SUBTASK 23-15-42-860-008 (2) Use the center Control Display Unit (CDU) to get access to the SAT USER ORT page. (a) Push the MENU key on the CDU keypad to show the CDU “MENU” page. (b) Select the LSK adjacent to <SAT to show the SAT-PHONE 1/2 page. (c) Push the NEXT PAGE key on the CDU keypad to show the SAT-PHONE 2/2 page. (d) Select the LSK adjacent to CONFIG> to show the SAT CONFIG 1/2 page. (e) Enter the USER ORT password “ICG220A2” into the CDU scratch-pad. NOTE: This is a password protected page. It is the operator's responsibility to record and to keep the password if it is revised. The SATCOM supplier may charge a fee for recovery of a lost password. 23-15-42 (f) Select the LSK adjacent to USER ORT> to show SAT USER ORT page. 1) Make sure that the CDU scratch-pad does not show one of these messages: a) NOT ALLOWED <1> If "NOT ALLOWED" message shows in the CDU scratch-pad, check the connection between the CDU and SYS 1 AIR/GND relay. b) INVALID PASSWORD <1> If "INVALID PASSWORD" message shows in the CDU scratch-pad, make sure that the password is correct. <2> If the problem continues, contact the SATCOM supplier. SUBTASK 23-15-42-210-003 (3) Examine the SAT USER ORT page. (a) Make sure that the settings are the same as the settings that show in the SAT USER ORT page (Figure 202). NOTE: If the SAT USER ORT setting(s) in the airplane are different from the graphic, revert to the default settings. SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-003 (4) Clear faults. (a) Press the CLEAR FAULTS> LSK to remove any fault indicators (Figure 203). SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-004 (5) Change the USER ORT Password via the CDU. (a) If it is necessary to change the default USER ORT password, do these steps: NOTE: This page allows the user to modify the default USER ORT password to a customized password. A customized password must be pre-approved by the airlines engineering with a valid airplane engineering order. 1) Type the current password into the CDU scratch-pad. 2) Select USER ORT>LSK. 3) Enter the new password (up to 8 digits with 0-9 and A-Z) into the CDU scratch-pad. 4) Select USER ORT>LSK. NOTE: The CDU will display SAVE IN PROG while saving on the left side of the scratch-pad. The CDU will display SAVE COMPLETE when the save is done. 5) Press SAVE>LSK to save changes. 6) Press <RESTART then <CONFIRM to reboot the CDU with the changed configuration. NOTE: It is operator's responsibility to record and to keep the password if it is changed. The SATCOM supplier may charge a fee for recovery of a lost password. SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-005 (6) Change the Aircraft Communications Addressing And Reporting System (ACARS) service provider via the CDU. (a) Enter the SAT USER ORT password in the CDU scratch-pad. (b) Select USER ORT>LSK to access the SAT UORT CONFIG page. (c) Press the <ACARS SRV LSK to toggle through the five available options (Figure 203): 23-15-42 1) GLOBALINK 2) ARINC DIRECT 3) HONEYWELL GDC 4) SITA 5) OTHER. (d) Press SAVE> LSK to save changes. NOTE: The CDU will display SAVE IN PROG while saving on the left side of the scratch-pad. The CDU will display SAVE COMPLETE when the save is done. (e) Press <RESTART then <CONFIRM to reboot the CDU with the changed configuration. SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-006 (7) Change the CALL DISPLAY via the CDU. NOTE: The CALL DISPLAY type shows either the name or number of the caller. When the <CALL DISPLAY is set BY NAME, the CDU will display the call recipient name if located in the directory for ATG calls. The caller user ID is displayed for GTA calls. NOTE: When the <CALL DISPLAY is set BY NUMBER, the CDU displays the number, which may be a phone number or short code. Speed dial numbers are not displayed on the CDU. (a) The default settings is the dataloaded value. To change the display settings, do these steps: 1) Enter the SAT USER ORT password in the CDU scratch-pad. 2) Select USER ORT>LSK to access the SAT UORT CONFIG page. 3) On the SAT UORT CONFIG page, press the <CALL DISPLAY LSK to toggle between the BY NUMBER and BY NAME options. 4) Press SAVE> LSK to save changes. NOTE: The CDU will display SAVE IN PROG while saving on the left side of the scratch-pad. The CDU will display SAVE COMPLETE when the save is done. 5) Press <RESTART then <CONFIRM to reboot the CDU with the changed configuration. SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-007 (8) Change Entry Control via the CDU. NOTE: The scratch-pad number entry control type has two possible configurations. ALL NUMBERS and SHORT CODES. When <ENTRY CONTROL is set to ALL NUMBERS, the CDU scratch-pad will accept long numbers, short codes, speed dial numbers and station extensions. NOTE: Selecting SHORT CODES does not permit any long numbers to be dialed. Speed dial numbers will be accepted in the scratch-pad. Station-to-station calling is enabled with both parameter settings. (a) The default settings is the dataloaded value. To change the display settings, do these steps: 1) Enter the SAT USER ORT password in the CDU scratch-pad. 2) Select USER ORT>LSK to access the SAT UORT CONFIG page. 3) On the SAT UORT CONFIG page, press the <ENTRY CONTROL LSK to toggle between the ALL NUMBERS and SHORT CODE options. 23-15-42 4) Press SAVE> LSK to save changes. NOTE: The CDU will display SAVE IN PROG while saving on the left side of the scratch-pad. The CDU will display SAVE COMPLETE when the save is done. 5) Press <RESTART then <CONFIRM to reboot the CDU with the changed configuration. SUBTASK 23-15-42-730-008 (9) If the SAT UORT CONFIG page is correct and no change needed, do these steps: (a) Press <RESTART then <CONFIRM to reboot the CDU and return to the SAT UORT CONFIG page. (b) This procedure is completed. F. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition SUBTASK 23-15-42-580-002 (1) Release the parking brakes.

SUBTASK 23-15-43-160-002 (1) Do these steps to install the O-ring [5]: (a) Apply a thin layer of AeroShell Grease 33, D50180, or Mobilgrease 33, D50306, to the O-ring [5] and O-ring groove. SUBTASK 23-15-43-860-003 (3) Put the antenna [1] in its installed position. (a) Make sure that the forward orientation label points to the front of the airplane. (b) Make sure that the four bolt holes on the antenna [1] align with the four bolt holes on the airplane structure. NOTE: Fasteners can be used to help align the gasket onto antenna and antenna onto fuselage. SUBTASK 23-15-43-860-004 (4) Do these steps to install three of the four mounting bolts [2] to attach the antenna [1] on the airplane: NOTE: Leave the last bolt (#4 bolt) out for the electrical bond check. (a) Apply a thin layer of Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, on the threads and shank to the three mounting bolts [2]. (b) Loosely install the three mounting bolts [2] to attach the antenna [1] on the airplane. (c) Tighten the three bolts [2] to 30.0 in-lb (3.4 N·m) with the torque wrench, STD-1014. SUBTASK 23-15-43-760-001 (5) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the antenna base and airplane structure (SWPM 20-20-00). NOTE: Use the empty mounting bolt hole to get access to the antenna base. (a) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550. (b) Make sure that the resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0 milliohm) or less. SUBTASK 23-15-43-860-005 (6) Do these steps to install the last mounting bolt [2] to attach the antenna [1] on the airplane: (a) Apply a thin layer of Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237, on the threads and shank to the mounting bolt [2]. (b) Tighten the bolt [2] to 30.0 in-lb (3.4 N·m) with the torque wrench, STD-1014. SUBTASK 23-15-43-860-006 (7) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name RYR 507, 508, 519-999; RYR 509-518 POST SB 737-23-1724 AND POST SB 737-23-1670 E 13 C04060 SATCOM SDU RYR 507, 508, 519-999; RYR 509-518 POST SB 737-23-1670 These circuit breakers are inoperative and should remain open: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name RYR 509-518 POST SB 737-23-1670 AND PRE SB 737-23-1724 E 13 C04060 SATCOM SDU (INOP) 23-15-43 RYR 509-518 POST SB 737-23-1670 AND PRE SB 737-23-1724 (Continued) (Continued) CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name RYR 507, 508, 519-999; RYR 509-518 POST SB 737-23-1670 E 15 C01422 SATCOM AURAL WARN (INOP) SUBTASK 23-15-43-390-002 (8) Do these steps to apply the weather/aerodynamic fillet seal in these areas: NOTE: Use of cotton plugs to cover all fastener heads is allowed. NOTE: Operators can defer the application of the aero-sealant in the antenna installation until next “A” check or overnight maintenance stop to prevent a flight delay. The deferral of the application of sealant must not exceed a maximum of eight weeks (SL 20-043). (a) Apply an aerodynamic fillet seal with sealant, A00247, to all the fastener holes (TASK 20-30-11-910-801). (b) Apply an aerodynamic fillet seal with sealant, A00247, around the base of the antenna (TASK 20-30-11-910-801). (c) Let sealant dry for the correct cure time. (d) If it is necessary, use the hardwood or plastic fillet smoothing spatula, STD-810, to make the surface smooth and remove unwanted material. (e) If it is necessary, use wiper, G50262, wet with isopropyl alcohol to make the surface smooth and remove unwanted material.

I. Installation Test

SUBTASK 23-15-43-860-007 RYR 507, 508, 519-999; RYR 509-518 POST SB 737-23-1724 AND POST SB 737-23-1670 (1) Do this task: Iridium SATCOM System - Operational Test, TASK 23-15-40-710-801. RYR 509-518 PRE SB 737-23-1724 AND POST SB 737-23-1670 (2) Airplanes with antenna and SDU wiring provisions only, installation test not required.

G. Underwater Locating Device (LF-ULD) Functional Test Using ULD Tester

SUBTASK 23-25-03-720-001 (1) Turn on the ultrasonic test set, COM-15616. (2) Connect the activation cord to the Universal Serial Bus (USB) receptacle of the ultrasonic test set, COM-15616. (3) Select the measurement button (triangle with a circle) on the ultrasonic test set, COM-15616 screen. (a) The connecting screen will appear on the ultrasonic test set, COM-15616 screen. NOTE: “ Reading incomplete retry in 66 sec.” error is displayed on the ULD tester screen if contact is lost during the measurement. “Check polarity retry in 66 sec.” error is displayed on the ULD tester screen if activation cord plug is not touching the proper polarity of the ULD. SUBTASK 23-25-03-720-002 (4) Touch the activation cord plug to the correct poles of the ULD and wait 3 minutes. (a) Make sure that the acoustic signal confirms successful completion and the data is displayed on ultrasonic test set, COM-15616 screen. (b) Make sure that on the right top of ultrasonic test set, COM-15616 screen that the Pulse symbol blinks simultaneously to transmitted pulse. (c) Make sure the battery voltage is not below 3.2 VDC. 1) If the voltage falls below 3.2 VDC, do this task: LOW FREQUENCY UNDERWATER LOCATING DEVICE (LF-ULD) - REMOVAL/INSTALLATION, PAGEBLOCK 23-25-03/401. SUBTASK 23-25-03-100-001 (5) Make sure that the water switch pins on the ULD has no grease or dirt. (a) If necessary, clean the switch pins with water, G50256 and general detergent, B50117.

Reference Description COM-1793 Multimeter - Digital/Analog (or equivalent meter meets task requirements) Part #: 117 Supplier: 89536 Part #: 260-8XPI Supplier: 55026 Part #: 287 Supplier: 89536 Part #: 289 Supplier: 89536 Part #: 87V Supplier: 89536 Part #: FLUKE 27 II Supplier: 89536 Part #: FLUKE-77-4 Supplier: 89536 Part #: WA2025 Supplier: 4MJL8 Opt Part #: 187 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: 189 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: 21 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: 27 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: 77 SERIES III Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: 87 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: FLUKE 27 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: MODEL 27 Supplier: 89536 STD-4931 Jumper - Electrical

D. Consumable Materials

E. Location Zones

Zone Area 111 Radome 23-25-03 F. Prepare for the Functional Test SUBTASK 23-25-03-010-003 WARNING USE CAUTION WHEN YOU OPEN THE NOSE RADOME COMPARTMENT . IF THERE IS A BATTERY FAILURE, A DANGEROUS GAS CAN EMIT . DO THE CORRECT EMERGENCY PROCEDURES IF YOU SMELL AN UNUSUAL ODOR AT THE RADOME AREA. (1) Do this task: Nose Radome - Open, TASK 53-52-00-010-802.

G. Low Frequency Underwater Locating Device (LF-ULD) Functional and Battery Test Using

SUBTASK 23-25-03-710-001 (1) Do an operational check of the ULD. (a) Do these steps to test the ULD in operation mode: 1) Connect the electrical jumper, STD-4931, wire ends to the ULD water switch pins for approximately three seconds. NOTE: The ULD contains an intelligent activation feature which avoids unintentional activation, whereby the durability of the battery could be impaired. 2) After three seconds, listen for the acoustic signal. 3) Remove the electrical jumper, STD-4931, and the acoustic signal will continue to pulse every ten seconds for 60 seconds. NOTE: The ULD is in the service operation mode for 60 seconds. The battery voltage is circulating to the water switch pins and the ULD is pulsating the ultrasonic signal. After 60 seconds, the ULD drops back into the sleep mode. SUBTASK 23-25-03-710-002 (2) Do these steps to check the ULD battery: (a) Adjust a digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, to the 20 VDC range and resistance of 10 MOhm. (b) Within 60 seconds, connect the probes of the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, to the ULD water switch pins. (c) Make sure that the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, displays a minimum of 3.2 VDC. NOTE: It is normal for the voltage fluctuate in the decimal range. The pulsing of the ultrasonic signal from the ULD causes a minor drop in voltage. The minimum value must not fall below 3.2 VDC. 1) If the voltage falls below 3.2 VDC, replace the ULD. (d) Remove the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793. (e) Make sure that the water switch pins on the ULD has no grease or dirt. 1) If necessary, clean the switch pins with water, G50256, and general detergent, B50117.

H. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 23-25-03-410-003 (1) Do this task: Nose Radome - Close, TASK 53-52-00-410-802.

23-27-32-470-801ACARS Software Installation with a Portable Data Loader (PDL) ATA 23 · Task 470-801

Task Description

3. ACARS Software Installation with a Portable Data Loader (PDL)

A. General

(1) This procedure tells you how to install software in the Aircraft Communications Addressing And Reporting System (ACARS) with a Portable Data Loader (PDL). (2) The ACARS management unit must contain these pieces of software: (a) Core and Application software. (b) Airplane Database software. (3) The Core and Application software must be installed before the Airplane Database software. (4) A PDL and a Control Display Unit (CDU) are necessary for this procedure. A data loader control panel and a PDL interface connector are also necessary. The data loader control panel is installed above the DATA TRANSFER UNIT RECEPTACLE connector on the P61 panel. (5) A PDL is not a Boeing supplied part. Refer to the PDL supplier for instructions for operation. The PDL will have a disk drive for software installation from disks. Some PDL have an internal mass storage device. If the software is stored in the PDL, then disks are not necessary. (6) Make sure the airplane is on the ground with the engines shutdown before you install the software. (7) Modern software loaders that meet the security requirements defined in ARINC 645-1 or newer are recommended to use. The procedures for modern software loaders will be marked as preferred for now and will be the only procedures available after removing floppy disk loaders procedures in the future. Please check with your Software Loader Supplier for current capabilities since this is still maturing across current and future software loaders.

B. References

Reference Title 20-15-11-400-801 On-Airplane Software - Installation (P/B 201)

C. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. 23-27-33-470-801ACARS Software Installation with a Portable Data Loader (PDL) ATA 23 · Task 470-801

Task Description

3. ACARS Software Installation with a Portable Data Loader (PDL)

A. General

(1) This procedure tells you how to install software in the Aircraft Communications Addressing And Reporting System (ACARS) with a Portable Data Loader (PDL). (a) The ACARS Communications Management Unit (CMU) must contain these pieces of software: 1) Core software 2) Operational software 3) Customer Unique Database software (b) The ACARS Operational software must be installed before the Customer Unique Database software. If the Operational software is already installed, then you can install only the Customer Unique Database software. (2) A PDL and a Control Display Unit (CDU) are necessary for this procedure. A data loader control panel and a PDL interface connector are also necessary. The data loader control panel is installed above the DATA TRANSFER UNIT RECEPTACLE connector on the P61 panel. (3) A PDL is not a Boeing supplied part. Refer to the PDL supplier for instructions for operation. A PDL has a disk drive for software installation from disks. Some PDL have an internal mass storage device. If the software is stored in the PDL, then disks are not necessary. (4) The airplane must be on the ground with the engines shutdown before you can install software. (5) Modern software loaders that meet the security requirements defined in ARINC 645-1 or newer are recommended to use. The procedures for modern software loaders will be marked as preferred for now and will be the only procedures available after removing floppy disk loaders procedures in the future. Please check with your Software Loader Supplier for current capabilities since this is still maturing across current and future software loaders.

B. References

Reference Title 20-15-11-400-801 On-Airplane Software - Installation (P/B 201)

C. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. 23-27-33 Reference Description COM-1915 Data Loader - ARINC 615 (without ARINC 645-1 Compliant) Part #: 11615-50 Supplier: 0D4J3 Part #: 2231560-1-B Supplier: 98571 Part #: 465130-01-01 Supplier: 30782 Part #: 800-0631 Supplier: 1JSZ6 Part #: CEI-715-DL-2 Supplier: 0BPH5 Part #: PXS-LDS-10 Supplier: 98571 Part #: YV68A110 Supplier: FAQ15 Opt Part #: 11615-02 Supplier: 0D4J3 Opt Part #: 11615-20 Supplier: 0D4J3 Opt Part #: 18000-02 Supplier: 0D4J3 Opt Part #: 30100 Supplier: 0BAW0 Opt Part #: 80000-03-01010203 Supplier: 0BAW0 Opt Part #: 80000-04-01020301 Supplier: 0BAW0 Opt Part #: 80000-05 Supplier: 0BAW0 Opt Part #: 964-0400-020 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 964-0400-024 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 964-0400-025 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 964-0400-030 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 964-0400-044 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 964-0400-045 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 964-0400-050 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 964-0400-054 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 964-0400-055 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: P2K-615A-06 Supplier: 0BAW0 COM-21223 Data Loader - ARINC 615 (with ARINC 645-1 Compliant) Part #: 403474 Supplier: CG603 Part #: 403562 Supplier: CG603 Part #: AE-615-MPDL Supplier: CD624 Part #: AE-DL-SMD Supplier: CD624 Part #: PDL-615-XT Supplier: 89Q20 Part #: PXS-LDS-12 Supplier: 98571

(b) Push the line select key (LSK) adjacent to the <DLK prompt on CDU-1. 1) Make sure that the DATALINK (DLK) MENU page shows on the CDU display. (c) Push the LSK adjacent to the MAINT> prompt. 1) Make sure that the CMU MAINTENANCE page shows on the CDU display. (d) Enter “SAM” in the CDU scratchpad. (e) Push the LSK 6R (blank key). 1) Make sure that CMU DEBUG page is displayed. (f) Push the LSK adjacent to VDRS. 1) Make sure that ACARS VDRS DEBUG menu is displayed. (g) Record the Transponder/Broadcast ICAO code (BC ICAO), shown at LSK 5R, for later use. • BC ICAO code: __________________. (h) Select the LSK adjacent to RETURN to go back to the DEBUG MENU page. (i) Select the LSK adjacent to the MAINT MENU. 23-27-35 (j) Enter “SERGE” into the CDU scratchpad. (k) Push the LSK 1R (blank key). 1) Make sure that the CMU INIT page is displayed. (l) Push the LSK adjacent to <AIRLINE ID. 1) Make sure that CMU AIRLINE ID INIT page is displayed. (m) Enter into the CDU scratchpad the airline ID. NOTE: Manual entry of Airline ID is required for the CMU. (n) Select LSK 2L MANUAL. (o) Push the LSK adjacent to <SELECT until the <SEL> indicator is adjacent to the LSK 2L. (p) Record the two letter Airline ID entered on the AIRLINE ID INIT page. • Airline ID: ___________. (q) Push the LSK adjacent to the RETURN until the CMU MAINTENANCE menu is displayed. (r) Push the LSK adjacent to the <APM prompt. 1) Make sure that the CMU APM MENU shows on the CDU display. (s) Push the LSK adjacent to the EDIT> prompt. 1) Make sure that the CMU APM EDIT page shows on the CDU display. (t) Use the CDU keypad to enter "THUYVAN" in the scratchpad. (u) Push the LSK adjacent to the boxes under the ENTER PASSWORD prompt. (v) Push the LSK adjacent to the CONTINUE> prompt. 1) Make sure that the CMU APM EDIT page 1 of 4 (1/4) shows on the CDU display. (w) Push the LSK adjacent to the line below the A/C TYPE prompt. 1) Make sure that the CMU AIRCRAFT TYPE shows on the CDU display. 2) Push the LSK adjacent to the <B737NG. 3) Push the LSK adjacent to the <RETURN. (x) Use the CDU-2 keypad to enter the registration number of the airplane as shown on the captain’s or first officer’s main instrument panel placard in the scratchpad. NOTE: Enter 7 characters. If the registration number is less than 7 characters, enter leading decimal points. Example: ..N1239 or .A-2345. NOTE: If the registration number is more than 7 characters, it is necessary to enter the registration number without the dash (-). Example: UPB3743. Examples of aircraft registration number verses scratchpad entry: DISPLAYED ON PLACARD ENTER IN SCRATCHPAD N1239 ..N1239 A-2345 .A-2345 N123456 N123456 UP-B3743 UPB3743 (y) Push the LSK adjacent to the line under the A/C REG prompt. 23-27-35 1) Make sure that the aircraft registration number displayed at the LSK adjacent to the A/C REG agrees with the registration number on the Captain's or First Officer's Main Instrument Panel placard. (z) Use the CDU keypad to enter the ICAO Address number of the airplane in the scratchpad. NOTE: The transponder/ICAO code was recorded at an earlier step. NOTE: The ICAO code must be converted to hexadecimal, which is 6 characters. Type the hexadecimal value of the ICAO number into the scratchpad. See W/D 34-53-11 or 34-53-21. (aa) Push the LSK adjacent to the line under the ICAO ADDR prompt. (ab) Push the LSK adjacent to the HFDR1 DLK prompt until NO HFDR1 DLK shows. (ac) Push the LSK adjacent to the HFDR2 DLK prompt until NO HFDR2 DLK shows. (ad) Push the NEXT PAGE function key. 1) Make sure that the CMU APM EDIT page 2 of 4 (2/4) shows on the CDU display. (ae) Push the LSK adjacent to the ARINC 750 line until CLASSIC 750 shows. (af) Push the LSK adjacent to VDR1 until VDR1 NOT INSTALLED shows. (ag) Push the LSK adjacent to VDR2 until VDR2 NOT INSTALLED shows. (ah) Push the LSK adjacent to VDR3 until VDR3 INSTALLED shows. (ai) Make sure that the A/C type is displayed at LSK 5L PDC as follows: 1) If B738 is not displayed then enter B738 in the scratchpad and select LSK 5L. (aj) Push the NEXT PAGE function key. 1) Make sure that the CMU APM EDIT page 3 of 4 (3/4) shows on the CDU display. (ak) Push the LSK adjacent to PRT , until PRT NOT INSTALLED shows. (al) Select CPDLC until NO shows. (am) Select AOC until NO shows. (an) Select 623 until NO shows. (ao) Push LSK 4L until SET FOR AOC ONLY shows. (ap) Push the NEXT PAGE function key on the CDU. 1) Make sure that the CMU APM EDIT page 4 of 4 (4/4) is displayed. (aq) Enter into the CDU scratchpad the airline ID, a forward slash “/” followed by the three character ICAO code. (ar) Select ACARS AIRLINE ID LSK 4L. (as) Select LSK 3R until CMU is displayed. (at) Select ATN VER (LSK 2L) until 41 is displayed. (au) Enter the 6 character hexadecimal value of the ADM code in the scratchpad of the MCDU (Table 201). Table 201/23-27-35-993-802 Example of the Hexadecimal ADM Code ADM CODE HEXADECIMAL ADM CODE NOS 4E454F (av) Select LSK 4L (ATN ADM). 23-27-35 (aw) Push the LSK adjacent to the STORE AND RESET> prompt. NOTE: The CMU will reset. This may take up to two minutes. (ax) Make sure that the <DLK prompt shows on the CDU MENU. (ay) When the <DLK prompt shows on the CDU display, make sure that the changes you made were saved. To verify the APM data, do this task: ACARS Airplane Personality Module (APM) Data Verification Check, TASK 23-27-35-700-801. 23-31-00-730-801Passenger Address System - System Test ATA 23 · Task 730-801

Task Description

3. Passenger Address System - System Test

A. General

(1) The system test has these test sections: (a) A ground mode test which checks the sound level of each passenger address speaker. (b) A flight mode test which makes sure that the sound level of the passenger address speakers increases when the engines are running. (2) This test makes sure the sound level of the speakers in the passenger cabin increases by at least 5 dBs when the engines are running. 23-31-00 (3) This test checks the sound level in the airplane with the engine running relay from the #1 engine, or the #1 and #2 engines, connected to the PA amplifier.

B. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. (e) Measure the sound level of the speakers: 1) Move the sound level meter across the same speaker panel used in the Prepare For Test section. 2) Write the value down. a) Make sure the value is at least 5 dBs higher than the first measurement. (f) Move the engine 2 start lever to the CUTOFF position. (g) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name D 4 C00459 ENGINE 2 IGNITION RIGHT D 6 C00151 ENGINE 2 IGNITION LEFT 23-31-00 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 3 C00360 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 2 (h) Measure the sound level across the same speaker. 1) Make sure that the sound level is approximately the same as in the first measurement. F. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition SUBTASK 23-31-00-860-002 (1) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name E 12 C00314 INDICATOR MASTER DIM SECT 2 F 11 C00317 INDICATOR MASTER DIM SECT 5 SUBTASK 23-31-00-860-003 (2) Do the steps that follow to put the airplane back to its usual condition: (a) Set the noise random noise generator, COM-1612, PWR/PTT switch to the OFF position. (b) Disconnect the noise random noise generator, COM-1612, from the microphone jack in the flight compartment. 1) Remove the test equipment from the airplane.
