Voice Recorder System Audio Fidelity Test SUBTASK 23-71-00-860-012 (1) Set the Cockpit Voice Recorder (CVR) AUTO/ON switch, on the P5 overhead panel, to the ON position. RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-506 NOTE: Both engines must be off for at least five minutes. RYR ALL (a) Make sure that the CVR AUTO/ON switch stays in the ON position. SUBTASK 23-71-00-710-015 (2) Do the operational test as follows: RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH HONEYWELL VOICE RECORDER (a) Push and hold the TEST switch, on the control panel for the voice recorder, for a minimum of 2 seconds. 1) Make sure that you hear a tone in the headphone. 23-71-00 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH HONEYWELL VOICE RECORDER (Continued) The STATUS light comes on. NOTE: If you hold the TEST switch down for more than 2 seconds, the CVR will continue in the test mode. a) A “PASS” Test is when the STATUS light is stable and bright. NOTE: You can apply a “PASS” Test if the STATUS light does this procedure. It must come on stable and bright for one second and then go OFF for a minimum of two seconds. b) A “FAIL” is when the STATUS light is not stable or the intensity decreases. <1> Do the applicable procedure to correct the system as given in the FIM. RYR ALL SUBTASK 23-71-00-860-013 (3) Put a cover on the area microphone for the voice recorder system. SUBTASK 23-71-00-710-001 (4) Do a test for the voice recorder microphone at the captain's position: (a) Connect a boom microphone (on-board boom microphone headset, STD-13898) to the captain's jack panel. (b) If the captain's ACP has a BOOM microphone switch, make sure that it is set to the BOOM position. (c) Speak into the boom microphone. 1) Make sure that you hear your voice through the headphone at the voice recorder control panel. NOTE: When you test the digital or solid state voice recorder you will hear your voice in the headset as you speak. When you test the analog or tape based voice recorder you will hear your voice in the headset after approximately a 0.5 second delay. (d) Disconnect the boom microphone from the captain's jack panel. SUBTASK 23-71-00-710-002 (5) Do a test for the voice recorder microphone at the first officer's position: (a) Connect a boom microphone to the first officer's jack panel. (b) If the first officer's ACP has a BOOM microphone switch, make sure that it is set to the BOOM position. (c) Speak into the boom microphone. 1) Make sure that you hear your voice through the headphone at the voice recorder control panel. NOTE: When you test the digital or solid state voice recorder you will hear your voice in the headset as you speak. When you test the analog or tape based voice recorder you will hear your voice in the headset after approximately a 0.5 second delay. (d) Disconnect the boom microphone from the first officer's jack panel. SUBTASK 23-71-00-710-017 (6) Do a test for the voice recorder microphone at the observer's position: (a) Connect a boom microphone or a hand mic, as applicable, to the observer's jack panel. 23-71-00 (b) If the observer's ACP has a BOOM microphone switch, make sure that it is set to the BOOM position. (c) Speak into the boom microphone or hand mic. 1) Make sure that you hear your voice through the headphone at the voice recorder control panel. NOTE: When you test the digital or solid state voice recorder you will hear your voice in the headset as you speak. When you test the analog or tape based voice recorder you will hear your voice in the headset after approximately a 0.5 second delay. (d) Disconnect the boom microphone or hand mic from the observer's jack panel. SUBTASK 23-71-00-700-003 (7) Remove the cover from the area microphone for the voice recorder system. SUBTASK 23-71-00-710-005 (8) Do a test for the area microphone of the voice recorder system: (a) Speak in an usual voice to the area microphone. NOTE: Keep 3 ft (0.9 m) to 4 ft (1.2 m) away from the area microphone for the voice recorder. 1) Make sure that you hear your voice through the headphone at the voice control panel. NOTE: When you test the digital or solid state voice recorder you will hear your voice in the headset as you speak. When you test the analog or tape based voice recorder you will hear your voice in the headset after approximately a 0.5 second delay. SUBTASK 23-71-00-860-014 (9) Do these steps to erase the memory for the voice recorder: (a) Set the airplane's parking brake. (b) Push the ERASE switch, on the voice recorder's control panel, for a minimum of one half second. 1) Make sure that you hear a tone through the headphone at the voice recorder control panel.

23-71-00-730-802Voice Recorder System - System Test ATA 23 · Task 730-802

Task Description

4. Voice Recorder System - System Test

A. General

(1) This procedure contains a task for the system test of the voice recorder system. RYR 507-999; RYR 013, 014 POST SB 737-23-1380; RYR 043, 044 POST SB 737-23-1484 OR POST SB 737-23-1483 SUBTASK 23-71-00-860-036 (2) Make sure that the ACARS system is serviceable (TASK 23-27-00-740-815-001 or TASK 23-27-00-740-814-009). H. Datalink Recording Interface Test

SUBTASK 23-71-00-720-001 (1) Do the steps that follow for the datalink recording interface test: (a) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name RYR 507-516 E 8 C01483 CMU-1 AC RYR 507-999 (b) Push and hold the “Test” switch, on the CVR control panel, for a minimum of 2 seconds. (c) Make sure that you can hear the tone in the headset. (d) Make sure that the LED status light DOES NOT illuminate, on the CVR control panel. NOTE: The LED might blink for less than a second but SHOULD NOT ILLUMINATE for more than a second or dimly glow continuously. (e) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name RYR 507-516 E 8 C01483 CMU-1 AC RYR 507-999 (f) Wait for 30 seconds. (g) Push and hold the “Test” switch, on the CVR control panel, for a minimum of 2 seconds. (h) Make sure that you can hear the tone in the headset. (i) Make sure that the LED status light illuminates, on the CVR control panel. NOTE: PASS/FAIL criteria: “PASS” if the LED illumination is stable and bright. “FAIL” if the LED illumination blinks, flickers, or dims. RYR ALL

I. CVR Engine Relay Test

SUBTASK 23-71-00-710-010 (1) Do a test for the CVR engine relay as follows: (a) Make sure that a headphone connected to the voice recorder's control panel. (b) Set the CVR AUTO/ON switch to the AUTO position. (c) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00458 ENGINE 1 IGNITION RIGHT A 3 C00153 ENGINE 1 IGNITION LEFT 23-71-00 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name D 4 C00459 ENGINE 2 IGNITION RIGHT D 6 C00151 ENGINE 2 IGNITION LEFT F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 3 C00360 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 2 B 4 C00359 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 1 (d) Set the left engine start lever to the IDLE position. 1) Make sure that the right engine start lever is in the CUTOFF position. 2) Wait approximately five minutes. NOTE: The CDS will toggle the engine running relay five minutes after the start lever is placed in the IDLE position. 3) Make sure that you can hear the flight deck conversation in the headphone. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH HONEYWELL VOICE RECORDER (e) Push and hold the TEST switch, on the voice recorder's control panel, for a minimum of 2 seconds. 1) Make sure that you hear a tone in the headphone. 2) Make sure that the STATUS light comes on once. RYR ALL (f) Set the left engine start lever to the CUTOFF position. 1) Wait approximately five minutes. NOTE: The voice recorder will continue to operate for five minutes after both engines have stopped running, or engine running simulation has been discontinued. 2) Make sure that you cannot hear the flight deck conversation in the headphone. (g) Set the right engine start lever to the IDLE position. 1) Wait approximately five minutes. NOTE: The CDS will toggle the engine running relay five minutes after the start lever is placed in the IDLE position. 2) Make sure that you can hear the flight deck conversation in the headphone. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH HONEYWELL VOICE RECORDER (h) Push and hold the TEST switch, on the voice recorder's control panel, for a minimum of 2 seconds. 1) Make sure that you hear a tone in the headphone. 2) Make sure that the STATUS light comes on once. NOTE: If the Aircraft Communications Addressing And Reporting System (ACARS) is disconnected, the status light does not illuminate. The tone in the headphone is the signal of a correct operation test. RYR ALL (i) Set the right engine start lever to the CUTOFF position. 23-71-00 1) Wait approximately five minutes. NOTE: The voice recorder will continue to operate for five minutes after both engines have stopped running, or engine running simulation has been discontinued. 2) Make sure that you cannot hear the flight deck conversation in the headphone. (j) Set the CVR AUTO/ON switch, on the P5 overhead panel, to the ON position. RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-506 NOTE: Both engines must be off for at least five minutes. RYR ALL 1) Make sure that the CVR AUTO/ON switch stays in the ON position. 2) Make sure that you can hear the flight deck conversation in the headphone.

J. Bulk Erasure Test

SUBTASK 23-71-00-710-013 (1) Do a test for the bulk erasure of the voice recorder as follows: (a) Make sure that the airplane wheels have chocks installed around them (CHOCK INSTALLATION, PAGEBLOCK 10-11-05/201). (b) Make sure that a headphone connected to the voice recorder's control panel. (c) Make sure that the CVR AUTO/ON switch, on the P5 overhead panel, is set to the ON position. NOTE: Both engines must be off for at least five minutes. WARNING MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL THE CHOCKS ON LANDING GEAR BEFORE THE RELEASE PARKING BRAKE. AIRCRAFT MOVEMENT CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT AROUND THE AIRCRAFT AND LANDING GEAR AREAS. (d) Release the parking brake. (e) Push the ERASE switch, on the voice recorder's control panel, for a minimum of one half second. 1) Make sure that you do not hear a tone in the headphone. (f) Set the parking brake. (g) Push the ERASE switch, on the voice recorder's control panel, for a minimum of one half second. 1) Make sure that you hear a tone in the headphone. WARNING MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL CHOCKS AT THE WHEELS. THE BRAKES WILL RELEASE WHEN YOU PUT THE AIRPLANE IN AIR MODE. THIS CAN CAUSE THE AIRPLANE TO MOVE SUDDENLY . IF YOU DO NOT INSTALL THE CHOCKS, INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (h) Do the PSEU BITE procedure to put the No. 1 air/ground system in air mode, do this task: Put the Airplane in the Air Mode, TASK 32-09-00-860-801. 23-71-00 (i) Push the ERASE switch, on the voice recorder's control panel, for a minimum of one half second. 1) Make sure that you do not hear a tone in the headphone. (j) Put the airplane back to the ground mode: 1) Do this task: Return the Airplane to the Ground Mode, TASK 32-09-00-860-802. (k) Push the ERASE switch, on the voice recorder's control panel, for a minimum of one half second. 1) Make sure that you hear a tone in the headphone.

K. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 23-71-00-860-027 (1) Disconnect the headphone from the voice recorder's control panel. SUBTASK 23-71-00-860-028 (2) Set the CVR AUTO/ON switch, on the P5 overhead panel, back to the AUTO position. SUBTASK 23-71-00-860-031 (3) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00458 ENGINE 1 IGNITION RIGHT A 3 C00153 ENGINE 1 IGNITION LEFT F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name D 4 C00459 ENGINE 2 IGNITION RIGHT D 6 C00151 ENGINE 2 IGNITION LEFT F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 3 C00360 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 2 B 4 C00359 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 1

NOTE: It is necessary to replace the removed Battery Code C with a new Battery Code C. Battery Codes B and D are interchangeable. NOTE: It is necessary to replace the removed Battery Code F with a new Battery Code F . Table 201/23-71-21-993-802 BATTERY CODE REQUIRED BATTERY KIT B 810-2007/K C 810-2008/K D 810-2007/K F 810-2042/K 23-71-21 (2) Install the ULB battery [28] (battery, G02440). NOTE: The Dukane 810-2007/K, 810-2008/K are 6 year lithium batteries used in the Dukane models DK120 and DK140 ULB. NOTE: The 810-2042/K are 7 year lithium batteries used in the Dukane model DK120-90 ULB. NOTE: Battery, O-ring and lubricant are provided in battery replacement kit. (a) Put a new battery replacement label [23] on the ULB [4] body. (b) On the date label, write the next scheduled replacement date for the new ULB battery [28] that you installed. NOTE: The date label is blank so you can write in a replacement date based on your maintenance schedule. CAUTION REMOVE ALL OF THE CONTAMINATION FROM THE THREADS AND THE O-RING GROOVES. CONTAMINATION CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE THREADS. THREAD DAMAGE CAN CAUSE LEAKS. (c) Clean the threads and the o-ring contact area in the ULB [4] body. CAUTION MAKE SURE THE POLARITY IS CORRECT . INCORRECT POLARITY CAN CAUSE PERMANENT DAMAGE TO THE BEACON. (d) Put the new battery [28] (battery, G02440) in the ULB [4] with the end identified INSERT THIS END in first. (e) Do these steps to test the beacon off-current: 1) Put the positive probe of a digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, on the positive end of the battery [28]. 2) Put the negative probe on the outer surface of the ULB [4]. 3) Make sure that the multimeter shows an electrical current of 3 microamperes or less. a) If the current is more than 3 microamperes, replace the ULB [4]. (f) Remove and discard the used o-ring [26] from the end cap [25]. CAUTION REMOVE ALL OF THE CONTAMINATION FROM THE THREADS AND THE O-RING GROOVES. CONTAMINATION CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE THREADS. THREAD DAMAGE CAN CAUSE LEAKS. (g) Clean the threads and the o-ring groove in the end cap [25]. (h) Apply a thin layer of lubricant, D50082, to the new o-ring [26] (O-ring, G50275), o-ring groove, and threads. (i) Install the new o-ring [26] (O-ring, G50275), on the end cap [25]. (j) Put the rubber shock cushion [27] smoothly on the end cap [25]. (k) Put the end cap [25] into the ULB [4] body. (l) Use a wrench, COM-1619, or spanner wrench, COM-15071, to tighten the end cap [25] until the cap flange touches the ULB [4] body. 23-71-21 1) Use only hand force on the wrench.

23-71-21-960-802Teledyne Benthos Underwater Locator Beacon Battery - Replacement ATA 23 · Task 960-802

Task Description

4. Teledyne Benthos Underwater Locator Beacon Battery - Replacement (Figure 204) NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

(1) This procedure contains the steps to replace the T eledyne Benthos ULB battery.

B. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-2543 Torque - Adapter, Used on Underwater Locator Beacon Part #: B362-09111 Supplier: 61257 Opt Part #: 008407 Supplier: 26858 Opt Part #: B362-04180A Supplier: 26858 STD-1066 Hose - Radiator, Split, 1-1/4 Inch Diameter, 5 Inch Length

C. Consumable Materials

D. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 23-71-21-860-001 (1) Measure the battery voltage of the ELP-362D ULB [4]. Use a high-impedance digital voltmeter with a minimum input impedance of 10 Megohms. (a) Put the negative meter lead on the water switch. (b) Put the positive meter lead on the bare aluminum surface of the beacon housing. (c) Read the voltmeter.

E. Removal Procedure

SUBTASK 23-71-21-510-002 (1) If the measured voltage is less than 6.0 Volts, send the ELP-362D ULB [4] to the manufacturer for servicing. SUBTASK 23-71-21-020-005 (2) If the measured voltage is 6.0 Volts or more, remove the battery [28] from the ELP-362D ULB [4]: CAUTION DO NOT HOLD THE ULB [4] WITH A VISE. THIS CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE ULB [4]. (a) Hold the ULB [4] body with a radiator hose - 1-1/4 Inch Diameter, 5 Inch Length, STD-1066. 23-71-21 (b) Use the underwater locator beacon torque adapter, COM-2543 to remove the end cap [25] identified as “BATTERY ACCESS”. (c) Turn the housing up to remove the battery [28] from the unit. (d) Discard the battery [28]. NOTE: Refer to local instructions when you discard the battery [28]. F. Installation Procedure SUBTASK 23-71-21-420-002 (1) Install the ULB battery, G50272 [28]: NOTE: The T eledyne Benthos C362–04270–2 is a five year battery. (a) Set the battery [28] until the arrow points to the top end of the unit. NOTE: The battery label has an arrow mark. (b) On the date label [23], write the next scheduled replacement date for the new ULB battery that you installed. NOTE: The date label [23] is blank so you can write in a replacement date based on your maintenance schedule. CAUTION INSTALL THE ULB BATTERY [28] CORRECTLY . INCORRECT POLARITY WILL CAUSE PERMANENT DAMAGE TO THE ULB [4]. (c) Put the new battery, G50272 [28] in the ULB [4] with the end identified INSERT THIS END in first. (d) Remove the o-ring [26] from its groove in the end cap [25]. CAUTION DIRT OR OTHER UNWANTED MATERIALS CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE THREADS AND THE O-RING SEAL. THIS CAN PERMIT WATER LEAKAGE. (e) Clean the O-ring groove of dirt, lint, and other unwanted materials. (f) Apply the O-ring lubricant to the new o-ring [26]. (g) Put the lubricated O-ring, G50273 [26] in the end-cap groove. (h) Attach the end cap [25] to the housing. (i) Use the underwater locator beacon torque adapter, COM-2543 to install the end cap [25] tightly. NOTE: Only use hand force on the underwater locator beacon torque adapter, COM-2543. (j) Torque the end cap [25] to 25 to 30 inch pounds.

23-71-21-700-801Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a 42A12 Test Set ATA 23 · Task 700-801

Task Description

6. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a 42A12 Test Set

A. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-10768 Test Set - 42A12, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Opt Part #: 42A12-1 Supplier: 94970

B. Procedure

SUBTASK 23-71-21-720-008 (1) If you have a 42A12 ULB Test Set, COM-10768, do this test of the ULB: NOTE: 42A12 can do a test for all ULBs. (a) Put the 42A12 ULB Test Set, COM-10768 as close as possible to the ULB. (b) Set the GAIN control switch on the 42A12 ULB Test Set, COM-10768 to the maximum clockwise position. NOTE: A background noise is heard. If you do not hear noise from the test set, replace the test set battery. (c) Set the TUNING control switch to the middle position. (d) Make sure that the 42A12 ULB Test Set, COM-10768 operates correctly. 1) Rub your thumb and fingers together in front of the microphone to make sure that it operates. NOTE: This will produce a rushing noise from the speaker. a) Make sure that you hear sounds through the speaker. (e) Use tape to attach a piece of wire, a shorting tab, or other conductive material to the ULB case and to the center of the water switch. NOTE: This will make a short circuit from the center of the water switch to the outer part of the ULB. (f) Set the GAIN control switch to a comfortable listening level. (g) Point the microphone of the test set towards the water switch end of the beacon for best results. 1) Make sure you hear a pulse tone. (h) Remove the wire, shorting tab, or other conducting material from the ULB case and the center of the water switch. (i) Set the GAIN control switch to the OFF position. (j) Make sure that the water switch on the ULB has no grease or dirt. (k) If necessary, do the steps that follow: 1) Clean the switch with water and detergent. 2) Dry the switch with a clean cloth.

23-71-21-700-802Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a PL1 Test Set ATA 23 · Task 700-802

Task Description

7. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a PL1 Test Set

A. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-10771 Test Set - PL1, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Opt Part #: PL1 Supplier: 94970

B. Consumable Materials

C. Procedure

SUBTASK 23-71-21-720-009 (1) If you have a PL1 ULB test set, COM-10771, do this test of the ULB: NOTE: PL1 can only do a test for the DK100 ULB. (a) Use Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270 to attach a piece of wire or other conductive material to the ULB case and the center of the water switch. NOTE: This will make a short circuit from the center of the water switch to the outer part of the ULB. (b) Put the end of the PL1 ULB test set, COM-10771 against the ULB, approximately one inch from the water switch. (c) Push and hold the operation switch on the PL1 ULB test set, COM-10771. 1) Make sure that the BEACON ACTIVE WHEN FLASHING light flashes. 2) Remove the piece of wire or other conductive material from the ULB case and the center of the water switch. 3) Make sure that the BEACON ACTIVE WHEN FLASHING light does not flash. (d) Release the operation switch on the PL1 ULB test set, COM-10771. (e) Remove the PL1 ULB test set, COM-10771. (f) Make sure that the water switch end of the ULB has no grease or dirt. (g) If necessary, do the steps that follow: 1) Clean the switch with water and detergent. 2) Dry the switch with a clean cloth.

23-71-21-700-803Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a PL3 Test Set ATA 23 · Task 700-803

Task Description

8. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a PL3 Test Set

A. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. 23-71-21 Reference Description COM-10772 Test Set - PL3, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Opt Part #: PL3 Supplier: 94970

B. Procedure

SUBTASK 23-71-21-720-003 (1) If you have a PL3 ULB test set, COM-10772, do this test of the ULB [4]: NOTE: PL3 can only do a test for the DK100 and DK120 ULBs. (a) Push and hold the PL3 ULB test set, COM-10772 against the ULB [4] water switch. 1) Make sure that you hear a pulse sound. 2) Make sure that you see the LED light comes on and off. (b) Remove the PL3 ULB test set, COM-10772. (c) Make sure that the water switch end of the ULB [4] has no grease or dirt. (d) If necessary, do the steps that follow: 1) Clean the switch with water and detergent. 2) Dry the switch with a clean cloth.

23-71-21-700-804Underwater Locator Beacon Test with an ATS-260 Test Set ATA 23 · Task 700-804

Task Description

9. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with an ATS-260 Test Set

A. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-978 Test Set - ATS-260, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Part #: ATS-260 Supplier: 1MXA1

B. Procedure

SUBTASK 23-71-21-720-004 (1) If you have a ATS-260 ULB test set, COM-978, do this test of the ULB [4]: NOTE: ATS-260 can only do a test for the ELP-362D ULB. (a) Put the ATS-260 ULB test set, COM-978 clip on the ULB [4]. (b) Push and hold the PUSH TO TEST button. (c) Put the ATS-260 ULB test set, COM-978 probe on the ULB [4] water switch. 1) Make sure that a green light (LED) shows. 2) Make sure that you can hear sounds from the ATS-260 ULB test set, COM-978 and/or the amber light (LED) flashes. (d) Release the PUSH TO TEST button. (e) Remove the ATS-260 ULB test set, COM-978. (f) Make sure that the water switch end of the ULB [4] has no grease or dirt. (g) If necessary, do the steps that follow: 1) Clean the switch with water and detergent. 23-71-21 2) Dry the switch with a clean cloth.

23-71-21-700-805Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a Seacom TS100 Test Set ATA 23 · Task 700-805

Task Description

10. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a Seacom TS100 Test Set

A. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-10769 Test Set - TS100, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Opt Part #: TS100 Supplier: 94970

B. Procedure

SUBTASK 23-71-21-720-005 (1) If you have a TS100 ULB test set, COM-10769, do this test of the ULB [4]: NOTE: TS100 can only do a test for the DK100 and DK120 ULBs. (a) Connect the probe head of the TS100 ULB test set, COM-10769 to the ULB [4] in its mount. (b) Slide the switch on the side of the TS100 ULB test set, COM-10769 housing to ON. 1) Make sure that the LCD display shows “TESTING”. (c) Press the button in the center of the TS100 ULB test set, COM-10769 to start a retest. 1) Make sure that the LCD display shows “TESTING”. (d) Within a few seconds, the LCD will change to show one of the following Pass / Fault messages: LCD Message Explanation Beacon Passed Beacon is operating properly Battery Fault Beacon is NOToperating properly No Pulse Output Beacon is NOToperating properly Pulse Fault Beacon is NOToperating properly Free-Run Fault Beacon is NOToperating properly Test Set Fault Test Set batteries must be replaced Need Service Beacon is NOToperating properly Open Probe/Batt. Probe head is not properly attached or the beacon battery is dead. 1) Make sure that the LCD shows “Beacon Passed”. NOTE: THE PASS / FAULT MESSAGE WILL BE DISPLAYED FOR APPROXIMATELY 10 SECONDS BEFORE RETURNING TO “READY FOR TEST”. (e) Remove the TS100 ULB test set, COM-10769. (f) Make sure that the water switch end of the ULB [4] has no grease or dirt. (g) If necessary, do the steps that follow: 1) Clean the switch with water and detergent. 23-71-21 2) Dry the switch with a clean cloth.

23-71-21-700-806Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS200 Test Set ATA 23 · Task 700-806

Task Description

11. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS200 Test Set

A. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-10770 Test Set - TS200, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Opt Part #: TS200 Supplier: 94970

B. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS200 Test Set

SUBTASK 23-71-21-720-011 (1) If you have a TS200 ULB test set, COM-10770, do this test of the Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB): NOTE: TS200 can do a test for all ULBs except Honeywell ULB DK290-11. Use Test Set TS500 for Honeywell ULB DK290-11. (a) Attach the test probe clip of the TS200 ULB test set, COM-10770, to the beacon in its mount. (b) Put the tip of the probe on the silver pad of the water switch at the end of the beacon. 1) The LCD display will show the battery voltage of the beacon. (c) Refer to the applicable battery code for the minimum permitted range of the beacon battery voltage: NOTE: Examine the battery replacement label to find the battery code. 1) Code A – 3.55 Volts 2) Code B – 2.97 Volts 3) Code C – 2.97 Volts 4) Code D – 2.97 Volts 5) Code F – 2.97 Volts RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH UNDERWATER LOCATOR BEACON PART NUMBER 266-E5542-00 6) Without battery code – 2.97 Volts. RYR ALL (d) Push the red button on the TS200 ULB test set, COM-10770. 1) The beacon starts and you hear a pinging noise from the TS200 ULB test set, COM-10770. (e) Remove the test probe clip of the TS200 ULB test set, COM-10770. (f) If it is necessary, replace the ULB. (g) Make sure that the water switch end of the ULB has no grease or dirt. (h) If it is necessary, do the steps that follow: 1) Clean the switch with water and detergent. 23-71-21 2) Dry the switch with a clean cloth.

23-71-21-700-807Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS500 Test Set ATA 23 · Task 700-807

Task Description

12. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS500 Test Set

A. General

(1) This procedure has steps to do a test of the Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) with a TS500 underwater locator beacon test set, COM-19623. NOTE: TS500 can do a test for all ULBs.

B. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-19623 Test Set - TS500, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Part #: TS500 Supplier: 94970

C. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS500 Test Set

SUBTASK 23-71-21-700-005 (1) Testing for the ULB battery voltage: (a) Attach the test probe clip of the TS500 underwater locator beacon test set, COM-19623 to the beacon in its mount. (b) Put the tip of the probe on the silver pad of the water switch at the end of the beacon. 1) The LCD display will show the battery voltage of the beacon. (c) Refer to the applicable battery code for the minimum permitted range of the beacon battery voltage: NOTE: Examine the battery replacement label to find the battery code. 1) Code F - 2.97 Volts (2) Testing for the operation of the ULB: (a) Keep the probes connected to the ULB. NOTE: The test set display will remain blank during this test. (b) Push the red button on the TS500 underwater locator beacon test set, COM-19623. 1) The beacon starts and you hear a pinging noise from the TS500 underwater locator beacon test set, COM-19623. 2) The TS500 underwater locator beacon test set, COM-19623 LED indicator will light up red every time the ULB pings. (c) Remove the test probe clip of the TS500. (3) If voltage or operation test does not pass, replace the ULB if necessary. (a) Make sure that the water switch end of the ULB has no grease or dirt. (b) If necessary, do the steps that follow: 1) Clean the switch with water and detergent. 2) Dry the switch with a clean cloth.
